</span></span><span id="S6.T4.3.6.2.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;"><span id="S6.T4.3.6.2.3.1" class="ltx_text"></span></span></th></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering" style="font-size:90%;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table">Table A4: </span>The table lists the update rules <math id="S6.T4.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(\tilde{w}_{1},\tilde{w}_{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>w</mi><mo mathsize="1.110em">~</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>w</mi><mo mathsize="1.110em">~</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(\tilde{w}_{1},\tilde{w}_{2})</annotation></semantics></math> associated to the linkage criteria of Table <a href="#S0.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> and that are used to efficiently update the interactions between clusters.</figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="S6.F6" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/1906.11713/assets/edge_contraction.svg" id="S6.F6.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_landscape" width="329" height="214"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S6.F6.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure A6</span>: </span><span id="S6.F6.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Example of edge contraction. First row: original graph <math id="S6.F6.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{G}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒢</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{G}</annotation></semantics></math>; clustering <math id="S6.F6.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Pi" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi mathvariant="normal">Π</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Pi</annotation></semantics></math> (gray shaded areas) with dashed edges on cut; cannot-link constraints (violet bars). Second row: contracted graph <math id="S6.F6.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mover accent="true"><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒢</mi><mo mathsize="1.110em">~</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">Π</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi}</annotation></semantics></math>. In step ii), edge <math id="S6.F6.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e_{uv}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e_{uv}</annotation></semantics></math> is contracted and node <math id="S6.F6.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> deleted from <math id="S6.F6.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mover accent="true"><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒢</mi><mo mathsize="1.110em">~</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">Π</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\Pi}</annotation></semantics></math>. In step iii), double edges <math id="S6.F6.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e_{tu}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>u</mi></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e_{tu}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.F6.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e_{tv}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e_{tv}</annotation></semantics></math> resulting from the edge contraction are replaced by a single edge with updated interaction.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Complexity</h4>

<div id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In the main loop of Phase 1, the algorithm iterates over all edges, but the only iterations presenting a complexity different from <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{O}(1)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{O}(1)</annotation></semantics></math> are the ones involving a merge of two clusters, which are at most <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N-1</annotation></semantics></math>. By using a union-find data structure (with path compression and union by rank) the time complexity of <span id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">merge<math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(u,v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(u,v)</annotation></semantics></math></span> and <span id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">find</span>(<math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math>) operations is <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{O}(\alpha(N))" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>N</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{O}(\alpha(N))</annotation></semantics></math>, where <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math> is the slowly growing inverse Ackerman function. The algorithm then iterates over the neighbors of the merged cluster (at most <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math>) and updates/deletes values in the priority queue (<math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{O}(\log|E|)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{O}(\log|E|)</annotation></semantics></math>).
Therefore,
similarly to a heap-based implementation of hierarchical agglomerative clustering, our implementation of GASP - Phase 1 has a complexity of <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{O}(N^{2}\log N)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>N</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{O}(N^{2}\log N)</annotation></semantics></math>. In the worst case, when the graph is dense and <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|E|=N^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|E|=N^{2}</annotation></semantics></math>, the algorithm requires <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{O}(N^{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{O}(N^{2})</annotation></semantics></math> memory. Nevertheless, in our practical applications the graph is much sparser, so <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{O}(|E|)=\mathcal{O}(N)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>N</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{O}(|E|)=\mathcal{O}(N)</annotation></semantics></math>.
With a single-linkage, corresponding to the choice of the <em id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Maximum</em> update rule in our framework, the algorithm can be implemented by using the more efficient Kruskal’s Minimum Spanning Tree algorithm with complexity <math id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{O}(N\log N)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒪</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>N</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{O}(N\log N)</annotation></semantics></math>, but only when cannotLinkConstraints are not used.
Moreover, GASP with <em id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Absolute Maximum</em> linkage can be implemented more efficiently (see next section).</p>
</div>
<figure id="S6.F7" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/1906.11713/assets/runtime-MWS.svg" id="S6.F7.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_landscape" width="685" height="343"></object>
<br class="ltx_break">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S6.F7.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure A7</span>: </span><span id="S6.F7.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Runtimes for different implementation of GASP with AbsMax linkage criterion. Runtimes are averaged over 5 runs.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px4" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Efficiency of different GASP implementations with AbsMax linkage criteria</h4>

<div id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In Fig. <a href="#S6.F7" title="Figure A7 ‣ Complexity ‣ A6.1 Implementation and complexity of GASP ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A7</span></a>, we compare the runtimes of three implementations of the AbsMax criteria: the implementation from <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite"><a href="#bib.bib74" title="" class="ltx_ref">wolf2019mutex</a></cite> for pixel graphs (<em id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Pixel-grid implementation</em>) and for general graphs (<em id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Efficient graph implementation</em>) as well as the HC implementation with AbsMax linkage (<em id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px4.p1.1.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Naive graph implementation</em>). The specialized implementations can exploit the properties of the underlying graph and are faster. But our generalization does not carry a large computational penalty and only requires a few extra seconds for partitioning graphs of a million nodes. Note that we have always used the most efficient implementation for the results reported in the paper. We will clarify this fact.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px5" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Median linkage</h4>

<div id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS1.SSS0.Px5.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We implemented median linkage in our library from the beginning but did not report on it in the main paper for two reasons: we consider the other criteria to span the range of interesting behavior well; and it performs no better than some of the other criteria (like average linkage) which are faster to evaluate.</p>
</div>
<figure id="S6.F8" class="ltx_figure"><img src="/html/1906.11713/assets/figs/counterexample_cropped.png" id="S6.F8.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_landscape" width="685" height="155" alt="Refer to caption">
<br class="ltx_break">
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S6.F8.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure A8</span>: </span><span id="S6.F8.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">GASP agglomeration with the Abs Max criterion: contracted edges are marked green. The last contraction increases the MC objective from -1 to 0.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
</section>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS2" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">A6.2 </span>GASP relation to the multicut objective</h3>

<div id="S6.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">For some of the linkage criteria, e.g. sum and average, GASP can be understood as a local search to the objective of the multicut optimization problem <a href="#S3.E1" title="Equation 1 ‣ 3.1 Notation ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>, see <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite"><a href="#bib.bib51" title="" class="ltx_ref">levinkov2017comparative</a></cite>. But this does not hold in general: the Abs Max linkage for example does not always decrease the MC objective (see counter example in Fig. <a href="#S6.F8" title="Figure A8 ‣ Median linkage ‣ A6.1 Implementation and complexity of GASP ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A8</span></a>).
Moreover, GASP cannot be seen as a k-approximation, because it is a polynomial algorithm and <em id="S6.SS2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Chawla, et al. Computational complexity, 2006</em>
has shown that approximating the multicut objective with any constant factor is in itself NP-hard.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS3" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">A6.3 </span>Proofs of Propositions <a href="#S3.Thmprop1" title="Proposition 3.1. ‣ AbsMax Linkage ‣ 3.3 GASP: New and existing algorithms ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.1</span></a>, <a href="#S3.Thmprop2" title="Proposition 3.2. ‣ Average, Single, and Complete Linkage ‣ 3.3 GASP: New and existing algorithms ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>, <a href="#S6.Thmprop1" title="Proposition A6.1. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A6.1</span></a>, and <a href="#S3.Thmprop3" title="Proposition 3.3. ‣ Algorithms defining an ultrametric ‣ 3.3 GASP: New and existing algorithms ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.3</span></a></h3>

<div id="S6.Thmtheorem1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="S6.Thmtheorem1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma A6.1</span></span><span id="S6.Thmtheorem1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="S6.Thmtheorem1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.Thmtheorem1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.Thmtheorem1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">If GASP Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.2 The GASP algorithm ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> with <span id="S6.Thmtheorem1.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Complete linkage criteria</span> enforces a constraint between two clusters in <em id="S6.Thmtheorem1.p1.1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_upright">Phase 1</em>, then the interaction between the clusters will never become positive over the course of the following agglomeration steps.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.2" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S6.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text">Two clusters are constrained in <em id="S6.SS3.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">Phase 1</em> only if their interaction is repulsive and, with complete linkage, the signed interaction between two clusters can only decrease over the course of the agglomeration. Thus, if two clusters are constrained by the algorithm, their negative interaction cannot increase and become positive later on in the agglomeration process.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.Thmtheorem2" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="S6.Thmtheorem2.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma A6.2</span></span><span id="S6.Thmtheorem2.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="S6.Thmtheorem2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.Thmtheorem2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.Thmtheorem2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">If GASP Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.2 The GASP algorithm ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> with <span id="S6.Thmtheorem2.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">AbsMax linkage criteria</span> enforces a constraint between two clusters in <em id="S6.Thmtheorem2.p1.1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_upright">Phase 1</em>, then the interaction between the clusters will never become positive over the course of the following agglomeration steps.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.3" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S6.SS3.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p2.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text">During the agglomeration the interaction between two clusters can only increase in absolute value. Thus, the negative interaction <math id="S6.SS3.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{W}{}(S_{i}\cup S_{j})&lt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∪</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{W}{}(S_{i}\cup S_{j})&lt;0</annotation></semantics></math> between two constrained clusters can possibly become positive over the course of next agglomeration steps only if there is at least another pair of clusters in the graph that has a positive interaction <math id="S6.SS3.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{W}{}(S_{l}\cup S_{t})&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>l</mi></msub><mo>∪</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{W}{}(S_{l}\cup S_{t})&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math> higher in absolute value: <math id="S6.SS3.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|\mathcal{W}{}(S_{l}\cup S_{t})|&gt;|\mathcal{W}{}(S_{i}\cup S_{j})|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>l</mi></msub><mo>∪</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo>&gt;</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∪</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|\mathcal{W}{}(S_{l}\cup S_{t})|&gt;|\mathcal{W}{}(S_{i}\cup S_{j})|</annotation></semantics></math>.
If such clusters <math id="S6.SS3.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S_{l},S_{t}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>l</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_{l},S_{t}</annotation></semantics></math> with positive interaction exist, we note that they must also be constrained (in the opposite case, the algorithm would have already merged them before to constrain <math id="S6.SS3.p2.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS3.p2.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_{j}</annotation></semantics></math>, because their priority is higher). In other words, a constrained negative interaction can become positive only if there is already another positive constrained interaction: but this can never be the case because initially all constrained interactions are negative.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.Thmtheorem3" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="S6.Thmtheorem3.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma A6.3</span></span><span id="S6.Thmtheorem3.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="S6.Thmtheorem3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.Thmtheorem3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.Thmtheorem3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">In the GASP Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 3.2 The GASP algorithm ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> with AbsMax or Complete linkage criteria (see linkage definition in Table <a href="#S0.T1" title="Table 1 ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>), the same final clustering is returned whether or not cannot-link constraints are enforced.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.4" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S6.SS3.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p3.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text">In phase 1 of Algorithm <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>, two clusters are merged only if the condition at line 9 is satisfied (i.e. when an interaction is both positive and not constrained). From Lemma <a href="#S6.Thmtheorem2" title="Lemma A6.2. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A6.2</span></a> and Lemma <a href="#S6.Thmtheorem2" title="Lemma A6.2. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A6.2</span></a> follows that with Complete and AbsMax linkage an interaction can never be both positive and constrained at the same time, so we directly conclude that the constrained and unconstrained versions of the algorithm will perform precisely the same agglomeration steps in phase 1.
In phase 2 (after constraints have been removed) no clusters are merged because all interactions are already negative (whether they previously constrained or not). Thus, both constrained and unconstrained versions of GASP return the same clustering <math id="S6.SS3.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Pi^{*}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">Π</mi><mo>∗</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Pi^{*}</annotation></semantics></math>.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p4.1" class="ltx_p">See <a href="#S3.Thmprop1" title="Proposition 3.1. ‣ AbsMax Linkage ‣ 3.3 GASP: New and existing algorithms ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.1</span></a></p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.5" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S6.SS3.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p5.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text">From Lemma <a href="#S6.Thmtheorem3" title="Lemma A6.3. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A6.3</span></a> it directly follows that GASP with AbsMax linkage criterion returns the same final clustering whether or not cannot-link constraints are enforced. In the following, we prove that MWS (see pseudocode <span class="ltx_ref ltx_missing_label ltx_ref_self"></span>) and the constrained AbsMax version of GASP also return the same clustering.
Both algorithms sort edges in descending order of the absolute interactions <math id="S6.SS3.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|w_{e}|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|w_{e}|</annotation></semantics></math> and then iterate over all of them. The only difference is that MWS, after merging two clusters, does not update the interactions between the new cluster and its neighbors.
However, since with an Abs. Max. linkage the interaction between clusters is simply given by the edge with highest absolute weight <math id="S6.SS3.p5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|w_{e}|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|w_{e}|</annotation></semantics></math>, the order by which edges are iterated over in GASP is never updated. Thus, both algorithms perform precisely the same steps and return the same clustering.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<figure id="S6.F9" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/1906.11713/assets/counter-example-GAEC.svg" id="S6.F9.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_square" width="329" height="363"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S6.F9.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure A9</span>: </span><span id="S6.F9.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;"> Counter-example showing that GAEC is not weight-shift invariant.
</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="S6.F10" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/1906.11713/assets/counter-example.svg" id="S6.F10.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_square" width="329" height="273"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S6.F10.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure A10</span>: </span><span id="S6.F10.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;"> Counter-example showing that HCC-Sum, MWS, HCC-Avg, and HCC-Single are not weight-shift invariant.
</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S6.SS3.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p6.1" class="ltx_p">See <a href="#S3.Thmprop2" title="Proposition 3.2. ‣ Average, Single, and Complete Linkage ‣ 3.3 GASP: New and existing algorithms ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a></p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.6" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S6.SS3.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p7.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p7.1.1" class="ltx_text">Theorem 1 in <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite"><a href="#bib.bib12" title="" class="ltx_ref">chehreghani2020hierarchical</a></cite> proves that hierarchical clustering with Average (HC-Avg), Single (HC-Single), and Complete linkage (HC-Complete) are weight-shift invariant.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.p8" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p8.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p8.1.1" class="ltx_text">The same is not true for GASP with Sum linkage criteria (GAEC and HCC-Sum), because by adding a constant <math id="S6.SS3.p8.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math> to all edge weights <math id="S6.SS3.p8.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{e}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{e}</annotation></semantics></math>, the interaction between two clusters <math id="S6.SS3.p8.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS3.p8.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> is increased by a factor <math id="S6.SS3.p8.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha|E_{ij}|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha|E_{ij}|</annotation></semantics></math>, which depends on the number of edges <math id="S6.SS3.p8.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|E_{ij}|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|E_{ij}|</annotation></semantics></math> connecting the two clusters. Thus, when all edge weighs of the graph are shifted, the agglomeration order may change. For a simple example of this, it is enough to consider the toy graph in Fig. <a href="#S0.F1" title="Figure 1 ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>a and shift the weights of the graph by <math id="S6.SS3.p8.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha=-3" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo rspace="0em">=</mo><mrow><mo lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha=-3</annotation></semantics></math> (see Fig. <a href="#S6.F9" title="Figure A9 ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A9</span></a>).</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.p9" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p9.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p9.1.1" class="ltx_text">The constrained versions of GASP (HCC-Avg and HCC-Single) are also not weight-shift invariant: here, the algorithm merges or constrains clusters in a given order, depending on the absolute interactions <math id="S6.SS3.p9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="|\mathcal{W}{}(S_{i}\cup S_{j})|" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∪</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">|\mathcal{W}{}(S_{i}\cup S_{j})|</annotation></semantics></math> between clusters; so, when edge weights are shifted by a constant <math id="S6.SS3.p9.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\alpha" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>α</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\alpha</annotation></semantics></math>, the sorting by absolute value can change arbitrarily together with the agglomeration order, as we show in the counter-example of Fig. <a href="#S6.F10" title="Figure A10 ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A10</span></a>.
Similarly, the Mutex Watershed algorithm is not weight-shift invariant because it uses a linkage criterion that compares weights by their absolute values (see again counter-example in Fig. <a href="#S6.F10" title="Figure A10 ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A10</span></a>.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.Thmprop1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_prop">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="S6.Thmprop1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Proposition A6.1</span></span><span id="S6.Thmprop1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="S6.Thmprop1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Consider a graph <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{G}(V,E,w_{e})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒢</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>e</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{G}(V,E,w_{e})</annotation></semantics></math>, a linkage criterion <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{W}{}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{W}{}</annotation></semantics></math>, and an agglomerative algorithm returning a binary rooted tree <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="T" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>T</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math> with height <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="h_{T}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>h</mi><mi>T</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h_{T}</annotation></semantics></math>. Then, <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(V,d_{T})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(V,d_{T})</annotation></semantics></math> defined in Eq. <a href="#S3.E3" title="Equation 3 ‣ Algorithms defining an ultrametric ‣ 3.3 GASP: New and existing algorithms ‣ 3 Generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> is an ultrametric if and only if the following is true:</span></p>

<p id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">In words, condition <a href="#S6.E7" title="Equation 7 ‣ Proposition A6.1. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a> means: if the algorithm merges nodes <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,v</annotation></semantics></math> before to merge nodes <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,t</annotation></semantics></math>, then the signed interaction <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m8" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mmultiscripts><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mprescripts></mprescripts><mi>T</mi><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,v)</annotation></semantics></math> between <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> has to be higher or equal than <math id="S6.Thmprop1.p1.m11" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,t)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mmultiscripts><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mprescripts></mprescripts><mi>T</mi><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,t)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>

<p id="S6.SS3.p10.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p10.2.1" class="ltx_text">In order to show that <math id="S6.SS3.p10.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(V,d_{T})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(V,d_{T})</annotation></semantics></math> is an ultrametric, we only need to prove the ultrametric property:</span></p>

</table>
<p id="S6.SS3.p10.3" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p10.3.1" class="ltx_text">When at least two of the three nodes <math id="S6.SS3.p10.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,v,t\in V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,v,t\in V</annotation></semantics></math> are the same, this property follows from Eq. <a href="#S6.E8" title="Equation 8 ‣ Proof. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">8</span></a> and Eq. <a href="#S6.E9" title="Equation 9 ‣ Proof. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">9</span></a>. When nodes <math id="S6.SS3.p10.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,v,t\in V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,v,t\in V</annotation></semantics></math> are distinct, from the definition of <math id="S6.SS3.p10.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="d_{T}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d_{T}</annotation></semantics></math> it follows that Eq. <a href="#S6.E11" title="Equation 11 ‣ Proof. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">11</span></a> is equivalent to:</span></p>
<table id="S6.E12" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math id="S6.E12.m1" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,v)\geq\min\{\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,t),\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(v,t)\}." display="block" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mmultiscripts><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mprescripts></mprescripts><mi>T</mi><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts><mo>≥</mo><mi>min</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mmultiscripts><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mprescripts></mprescripts><mi>T</mi><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts><mo>,</mo><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒲</mi><mmultiscripts><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mprescripts></mprescripts><mi>T</mi><mrow></mrow></mmultiscripts><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,v)\geq\min\{\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(u,t),\mathcal{W}{}_{T}(v,t)\}.</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td>
<td rowspan="1" class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_eqno ltx_align_middle ltx_align_right"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(12)</span></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="S6.SS3.p10.4" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p10.4.1" class="ltx_text">In the following, we prove both sides of the <em id="S6.SS3.p10.4.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">if and only if</em> statement in the proposition. First, we prove the <math id="S6.SS3.p10.m6" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="(\Leftarrow)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em" stretchy="false">⇐</mo><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(\Leftarrow)</annotation></semantics></math> side, i.e. that if assumption <a href="#S6.E7" title="Equation 7 ‣ Proposition A6.1. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a> holds, then <math id="S6.SS3.p10.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(V,d_{T})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>T</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(V,d_{T})</annotation></semantics></math> is an ultrametric and <a href="#S6.E12" title="Equation 12 ‣ Proof. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">12</span></a> holds.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.p11" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p11.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p11.1.1" class="ltx_text">Case 1: in Eq. <a href="#S6.E12" title="Equation 12 ‣ Proof. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">12</span></a>, <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t\in V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t\in V</annotation></semantics></math> is part of the sub-tree <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="{T[u\vee v]}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{T[u\vee v]}</annotation></semantics></math>. In other words, the algorithm first merges node <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> with either node <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> or <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>, and then <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> are merged together. Let us assume that <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> is first merged with <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> (the following proof also holds for the opposite case in which <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> is first merged with <math id="S6.SS3.p11.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>):</span></p>

</div>
<div id="S6.SS3.p12" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS3.p12.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS3.p12.1.1" class="ltx_text">Case 2: in Eq. <a href="#S6.E12" title="Equation 12 ‣ Proof. ‣ A6.3 Proofs of Propositions , , , and ‣ A6 Appendix ‣ GASP, a generalized framework for agglomerative clustering of signed graphs and its application to Instance Segmentation" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">12</span></a>, <math id="S6.SS3.p12.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t\in V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t\in V</annotation></semantics></math> is <em id="S6.SS3.p12.1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">not</em> part of the sub-tree <math id="S6.SS3.p12.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="{T[u\vee v]}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∨</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{T[u\vee v]}</annotation></semantics></math>. Thus, the algorithm first merges nodes <math id="S6.SS3.p12.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS3.p12.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>, and then it merges node <math id="S6.SS3.p12.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> together with the cluster containing <math id="S6.SS3.p12.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS3.p12.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>:</span></p>
